{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that every irreducible integer quartic with no fixed prime-square divisor takes squarefree values with the predicted positive Euler-product density. More generally, we obtain the corresponding $`(d-2)`$-power-free density in degrees $`4\\le d\\le8`$. The proof combines number-field factorization, determinant estimates with adaptive auxiliary primes, and explicit low-degree geometry. Together with Browning's theorem for higher degrees, this gives the $`(d-2)`$-power-free density for every <i>d</i> ≥ 4.",

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every irreducible integer quartic with no fixed prime-square divisor takes squarefree values with the predicted positive Euler-product density. More generally, we obtain the corresponding $`(d-2)`$-power-free density in degrees $`4\le d\le8`$. The proof combines number-field factorization, determinant estimates with adaptive auxiliary primes, and explicit low-degree geometry. Together with Browning's theorem for higher degrees, this gives the $`(d-2)`$-power-free density for every <i>d</i> ≥ 4.

- **claim-low-degree-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-density)): Let $f\in\mathbb Z[x]$ be irreducible over $\mathbb Q$, of degree $d$ with $4\le d\le8$, and put $k=d-2$. If <a href="#eq:local-admissibility" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-admissibility">[eq:local-admissibility]</a> holds, then $$S_{f,k}(X)=c_{f,k}X+o_f(X),\qquad c_{f,k}=\prod_p\left(1-\frac{\rho_f(p^k)}{p^k}\right)>0.$$ No primitivity or sign condition on the leading coefficient is required.
  - Explanation: Local admissibility gives the predicted positive density of power-free values for each fixed polynomial in the stated degree range. The density retains the local factors of the original polynomial.
- **claim-all-degrees-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-all-degrees)): Let $f\in\mathbb Z[x]$ be irreducible over $\mathbb Q$ of degree $d\ge4$, and put $k=d-2$. If $f$ has no fixed prime $k$th-power divisor, then $$S_{f,k}(X)=c_{f,k}X+o_f(X),\qquad c_{f,k}=\prod_p\left(1-\frac{\rho_f(p^k)}{p^k}\right)>0.$$
  - Explanation: The low-degree theorem and the cited higher-degree theorem together give the power-free density throughout the stated degree range.
- **claim-separable-and-simultaneous-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-separable-products)): Fix an integer $k\ge2$. Let $F\in\mathbb Z[x]\setminus\{0\}$ be separable over $\mathbb Q$, with every nonconstant irreducible factor of degree at most $k+2$. If $\rho_F(p^k)<p^k$ for every prime $p$, then $$S_{F,k}(N)=c_{F,k}N+o_{F,k}(N),\qquad c_{F,k}=\prod_p\left(1-\frac{\rho_F(p^k)}{p^k}\right)>0.$$ More generally, let $\mathbf F=(F_1,\ldots,F_r)$, with $r\ge1$, be a fixed family of nonzero integer polynomials, each separable over $\mathbb Q$ with all nonconstant irreducible factors of degree at most $k+2$. Factors may be shared by different $F_i$. Put $$\Omega_p=\{a\in\mathbb Z/p^k\mathbb Z:p^k\mid F_i(a)\text{ for at least one }i\}.$$ Let $S_{\mathbf F,k}(N)$ count the integers $1\le n\le N$ for which every $F_i(n)$ is $k$-power-free. If $\#\Omega_p<p^k$ for every prime $p$, then $$S_{\mathbf F,k}(N)=c_{\mathbf F,k}N+o_{\mathbf F,k}(N),\qquad c_{\mathbf F,k}=\prod_p\left(1-\frac{\#\Omega_p}{p^k}\right)>0.$$
  - Explanation: The result treats power-freeness of a separable product and simultaneous power-freeness of a fixed family. The simultaneous density uses the union of forbidden local classes, including shared factors.
- **claim-large-prime-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-large-prime-tail)): Let $f\in\mathbb Z[x]$ be primitive and irreducible, with positive leading coefficient and degree $4\le d\le8$. Put $k=d-2$. There exists $\delta=\delta_f>0$ such that $$\#\{n\in\mathbb Z:X<n\le2X,\ p^k\mid f(n) \text{ for some prime }p>X\} \ll_f X^{1-\delta}.$$
  - Explanation: Inputs whose polynomial values have a sufficiently large prime-power divisor form a set with a power saving. Local admissibility is not needed for this tail estimate.
- **claim-sieve-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-sieve-transfer)): Let $f\in\mathbb Z[x]$ be irreducible over $\mathbb Q$, of degree $d\ge2$, and let $k\ge2$. Suppose that $\rho_f(p^k)<p^k$ for every prime $p$. Write $$f=s c g,\qquad s\in\{-1,1\},\quad c\in\mathbb N,$$ where $c$ is the positive content of $f$ and $g\in\mathbb Z[x]$ is primitive with positive leading coefficient. Assume that $$T_g(X):=\#\{n\in\mathbb Z:X<n\le2X,\ p^k\mid g(n) \text{ for some prime }p>X\}=o_{g,k}(X). \label{eq:sieve-tail-hypothesis}$$ Then $$S_{f,k}(N)=c_{f,k}N+o_{f,k}(N),\qquad c_{f,k}=\prod_p\left(1-\frac{\rho_f(p^k)}{p^k}\right)>0.$$
  - Explanation: A negligible large-prime tail for the primitive positive-leading part suffices for the exact density of the original polynomial. Content primes remain in the finite sieve.
- **claim-sign-content-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-density-content)): Let $f\in\mathbb Z[x]$ be irreducible over $\mathbb Q$, of degree at least two, and let $k\ge2$. Suppose that $\rho_f(p^k)<p^k$ for every prime $p$, and write $f=s c g$, where $s\in\{-1,1\}$, $c$ is the positive content, and $g$ is primitive with positive leading coefficient. If $$S_{g,k}(N)=c_{g,k}N+o_{g,k}(N),\qquad c_{g,k}=\prod_p\left(1-\frac{\rho_g(p^k)}{p^k}\right),$$ then $S_{f,k}(N)=c_{f,k}N+o_{f,k}(N)$ with the Euler product $c_{f,k}>0$ of Proposition <a href="#prop:sieve-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:sieve-transfer">5</a>.
  - Explanation: An existing density theorem for the primitive part transfers across sign and content without requiring a separate theorem in arithmetic progressions.
- **claim-uniform-affine-counting** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-affine-counting)): Fix integers $1\le r\le N$, a degree bound $e_0$, a constant $C$, positive coordinate weights $\omega_l$, and positive numbers $\delta_1,\ldots,\delta_r$. For $X\ge2$, let $S\subseteq\mathbb Z^N$ satisfy $$\|z_l\|\le C X^{\omega_l}\quad(z\in S),$$ and suppose $S$ lies on an algebraic set $Z$ of degree at most $e_0$ and dimension at most $r$. Assume the following uniform alternative. For every fixed degree bound $e$, each irreducible subvariety $A\subseteq Z$ defined over $\mathbb Q$, of degree at most $e$ and dimension $h>0$, meeting $S$, satisfies either $$\label{eq:affine-exception} \|S\cap A\|\ll_{e,\alpha} X^\alpha \quad\text{for every fixed }\alpha>0,$$ or $$\label{eq:affine-hilbert-input} H_A(T)\ge \frac{T^h}{h!\delta_h^h}-C_eT^{h-1} \quad(T\ge1).$$ The constants in these alternatives are uniform in $X,Z,S,A$. Put $E_h=\max_{h\le s\le r}\delta_s$. Then, for every $\varepsilon>0$, $$\label{eq:affine-counting-conclusion} \|S\|\ll_\varepsilon X^{E_1+\cdots+E_r+\varepsilon}.$$ The implied constant depends only on the fixed data and the uniform constants in the hypotheses. In particular, no bound on the coefficients initially defining $Z$ is required.
  - Explanation: Uniform Hilbert lower bounds or exceptional small-count alternatives on all relevant subvarieties control the total integer-point count. Adaptive prime choices permit the initial equations to vary without a coefficient-height bound.
- **claim-controlled-affine-patches** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-affine-patches)): Let $S$ satisfy the box bound in Theorem <a href="#thm:affine-counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:affine-counting">8</a>. For every algebraic set $Z'\subseteq\mathbb C^N$ of degree at most $e$, there is a list of boundedly many patches $(A,G,\Delta)$ with $A\subseteq Z'$ that covers $S\cap Z'$. Their degrees are bounded, and all coefficients of $G$ and $\Delta$ are bounded by $C'X^{C'}$, where the bounds depend only on $e,N,C$ and the weights. In particular, at a covered point $z$, the integer $\Delta(z)$ is nonzero and has absolute value at most $C''X^{C''}$ with the same uniform dependence. The construction can be fixed as a function of $Z'$ and $S$ alone.
  - Explanation: The finite integer set admits a deterministic cover by local smooth patches of controlled degree and height. Proper intersections handle points missed by a chosen Jacobian minor.
- **claim-simultaneous-residue-cut** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-affine-residue-cut)): Fix $\nu>0$ and a bound on the degree of a patch $(A,G,\Delta)$ and its equations, with $h=\dim A>0$. Suppose $A$ satisfies <a href="#eq:affine-hilbert-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:affine-hilbert-input">[eq:affine-hilbert-input]</a>. Let $z\in S\cap A$ be covered by the patch, and let $\mathcal Q$ be distinct primes such that $$q\nmid\Delta(z)\quad(q\in\mathcal Q),\qquad \prod_{q\in\mathcal Q}q\ge X^{\delta_h+\nu}.$$ For sufficiently large $X$, the set of all points in $S\cap A$ congruent to $z$ modulo these primes lies on a polynomial of bounded degree that does not vanish identically on $A$. The degree bound is uniform in the patch and primes, with dependence on $\nu$ and the fixed data. Additional congruence conditions may also be imposed.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently large product of primes avoiding the patch minor makes evaluation determinants vanish and supplies a proper polynomial cut of the full simultaneous residue class.
- **claim-adaptive-prime-deletion** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-affine-deletion)): Fix $z\in S$, all blocks except the $i$-th, and a prime pool $\mathcal P_i$ disjoint from those blocks, whose primes lie between $X^\zeta$ and $C_rX^\zeta$, where $\zeta>0$ is fixed and $\|\mathcal P_i\|\longrightarrow\infty$. Choose $t$ uniformly among $T_0$ consecutive positive integers $\ell,\ldots,\ell+T_0-1$, fixed independently of $X$, and then choose $\mathcal B_i$ uniformly among the $t$-subsets of the pool. The expected fraction of primes in $\mathcal B_i$ dividing at least one of the full-path integers $\Delta_0(z),\ldots,\Delta_r(z)$ is at most $$B_0/T_0+o(1).$$ The error is uniform in $z$ and in the fixed other blocks.
  - Explanation: Deleting a sampled prime compares deterministic rank records. Rank stability preserves the minors, and the remaining divisibility probability is small because the deleted-path minors have polynomial size.
- **claim-balanced-number-field-factors** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-balanced-factorization)): There are a positive integer $\mu$ and a fixed finite list of integral ideals $\mathfrak c$ of $K$ with the following property. For all sufficiently large $X$, each pair $(n,p)$ above admits integral elements $\alpha,\beta\in\mathcal O_K$ and one ideal from the list such that $$\label{eq:arithmetic-factorization} \alpha^k\beta=\mu(n-\theta),\qquad \|N_{K/\mathbb Q}(\alpha)\|=pN(\mathfrak c),$$ and, for every embedding $\sigma_i$, $$\label{eq:arithmetic-balanced-sizes} \|\sigma_i(\alpha)\|\asymp_f P^{1/d},\qquad \|\sigma_i(\beta)/\mu\|\asymp_f X/P^{k/d}.$$ We may select one representation for each $n$ under consideration. For a fixed ideal case and fixed $\alpha$, there are at most $d$ selected values of $n$.
  - Explanation: Ideal-class corrections make the chosen prime ideal principal, and units balance its generator across embeddings. The selected representations have bounded multiplicity for a fixed first factor.
- **claim-paired-analytic-boxes** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-paired-boxes)): Fix $0<w<1$ and an integer $M\asymp X^w$. Partition each of the $s$- and $t$-cubes into grid boxes of side $M^{-1}$. For the selected points in any fixed arithmetic and coordinate chart, the following hold uniformly.* 1. *The vector $t$ is locally an analytic function of $(s,z)$. Conversely, $s$ is locally given by boundedly many analytic branches in $(t,z)$.* 2. *The branches needed at a counted point extend to a neighborhood of fixed radius containing the corresponding grid base with last coordinate $z=0$. Derivatives of every fixed order are uniformly bounded there.* 3. *Every relevant box of either type has $O_f(1)$ partner boxes of the other type. In particular, there are $O_f(M^{d-1})$ relevant pairs of boxes.* *When $m=(d-1)w$, the last bound is $O_f(X^m)$. No primality assumption on $M$ is needed for this lemma.
  - Explanation: Balanced conjugate forms permit analytic forward coordinates and finitely many reverse branches. Uniform derivatives make the partner relation between the direction grids bounded in both directions.
- **claim-prime-bicone-hilbert-polynomial** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bicone)): The defining ideal of $\mathcal B$ is prime and is a complete intersection of $d-2$ equations of bidegree $(k,1)$. The dimension of $\mathcal V$ is $d$. If $A,B$ tend to infinity with fixed positive rational ratio $B/A$, its bihomogeneous coordinate space in degree $(A,B)$ has dimension $$R(A,B)+O(A^{d-1}),$$ where $$R(A,B)=\frac1{d!}\sum_{j=1}^{d-1} \binom dj\binom{d-2}{j-1}k^{j-1}A^{d-j}B^j. \label{eq:hilbert-polynomial}$$
  - Explanation: The product relations define a prime complete intersection. Its Hilbert series yields an explicit leading bihomogeneous dimension polynomial.
- **claim-archimedean-mixed-and-pure-cuts** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-archimedean-cuts)): Fix positive upper exponents $\eta_+,b_+$. Consider a paired box as in Lemma <a href="#lem:paired-boxes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:paired-boxes">13</a>, with integer coordinates satisfying $$\|x_i\|\ll X^{\eta_+/d},\qquad \|y_i\|\ll X^{b_+/d}.$$ Assume that its normalized chart variables have width $O(X^{-w})$, that $z=1/n=O(X^{-1})$, and that the forward and reverse analytic chart functions have the uniform derivative bounds of that lemma. Let $t_*,w>0$ be fixed rational numbers satisfying $$\begin{split} w^{d-1}H(t_*)&> \left(\frac{(d+1)(\eta_++b_+t_*)}{d^2}\right)^d,\\ w^{d-2}\min\{H_a(t_*),H_b(t_*)\}&> \left(\frac{\eta_++b_+t_*}{d-1}\right)^{d-1}. \end{split} \label{eq:cut-conditions}$$ For sufficiently large $X$, the points in this box lie on a bounded number of bounded-degree algebraic sets of the following two types:* 1. *a mixed hypersurface section of $\mathcal V$, whose bicone is a prime complete intersection with one additional equation of bidegree $(\lambda,s_0)$, where $\lambda,s_0\geq1$;* 2. *a set of biprojective dimension at most $d-2$.* *On returning to the affine equations $a_i^kb_i=n-\theta_i$, the corresponding dimensions are $d$ and at most $d-1$. All number and degree bounds are uniform over the boxes and $X$.
  - Explanation: The first determinant produces a section. Mixed components retain a controlled complete-intersection description, while pure components receive an additional determinant cut using their exact coordinate relation.
- **claim-lattice-basis-coordinate-control** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-lattice-basis)): Let $\Lambda\subset\mathbb R^d$ be a full lattice of covolume $\Delta$, and let $\lambda_1$ be its shortest nonzero Euclidean length. There is a lattice basis $b_1,\ldots,b_d$ with $$\prod_{i=1}^d\\|b_i\\|\le C_d\Delta.$$ In this basis, every $v\in\Lambda$ with $\\|v\\|\le C$ has integral coordinates of absolute value at most $CC_d/\lambda_1$.
  - Explanation: A basis with controlled product of lengths converts a Euclidean bound into an integral-coordinate bound governed by the shortest lattice vector.
- **claim-paired-lattice-box-ranges** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-lattice-boxes)): For the paired boxes of Lemma <a href="#lem:paired-boxes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:paired-boxes">13</a>, assume the coordinate bounds above and fix parameters satisfying <a href="#eq:lattice-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lattice-parameters">[eq:lattice-parameters]</a>. The paired boxes can be grouped into finitely many ranges indexed by $j=\ell\tau$, $\ell\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}$, with $j\le m/d$. The number of paired boxes in range $j$ is $$\label{eq:lattice-box-count} O\bigl(X^{m-dj}\bigr).$$ For each paired box there are integral unimodular changes $x\leftrightarrow x'$ and $y\leftrightarrow y'$ such that all its selected points satisfy $$\label{eq:lattice-coordinate-bounds} \|x'_i\|\ll X^U,\qquad \|y'_i\|\ll X^V, \qquad U=u+j+\tau,\quad V=v+j+\tau.$$ The matrices of these changes and their inverses have entries bounded by a fixed power of $X$.
  - Explanation: Short lattice vectors enlarge the transformed coordinate bounds but also reduce the number of possible direction boxes. A common excess parameter organizes this tradeoff for both blocks.
- **claim-quartic-infinite-fiber-removal** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-quartic-fibers)): Suppose $d=4$, and let $Z$ be an initial bounded-degree set from Proposition <a href="#prop:archimedean-cuts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:archimedean-cuts">15</a>, restricted to the open $a_1a_2a_3a_4\ne0$. Use coordinates $(a,n)$ there, with $b_i=(n-\theta_i)/a_i^2$. Assume either that $\dim Z\le3$, or that $Z$ has the irreducible equation $F(a,n)=0$ with positive degree in $n$. The set of $a$ for which the $n$-fiber has dimension one has a closure of dimension at most two and bounded degree.* *In a box satisfying <a href="#eq:lattice-coordinate-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:lattice-coordinate-bounds">[eq:lattice-coordinate-bounds]</a>, deleting all selected points above this base costs $O_f(X^{2U})$ points. On every irreducible subvariety of $Z$ meeting the remaining selected set, $n$ is algebraic over the field generated by the $a_i$.
  - Explanation: Removing the low-dimensional base of infinite input fibers forces the input coordinate to be algebraic over the first-block field on all remaining relevant subvarieties.
- **claim-selected-pair-weighted-hilbert** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-weighted-hilbert)): For each nonzero coefficient polynomial $f_I$, define $$\nu_{i,I}=\operatorname{ord}_{n-\theta_i}f_I,\qquad e_i=\min_I(I_i+k\nu_{i,I}),$$ and put $$D=\max_I\{U\|I\|+(kU+V)\deg f_I\}-U\sum_i e_i. \label{eq:extracted-weight}$$ Then $D>0$, and the weighted filtered Hilbert function of the pair image satisfies $$H_{Y_{\mathrm{pair}}}(T)= \frac{(kU+V)^{h-1}D}{U^hV^h}\frac{T^h}{h!} +O(T^{h-1}+1). \label{eq:pair-hilbert}$$ This is a lower bound for $H_Y(T)$. If the function field degree of $Y$ over its pair image is at least two, the leading coefficient in this lower bound can be doubled. For fixed weights and bounded degree of $Y$, all error constants are uniform, independently of the coefficients of its equations.
  - Explanation: Extraction of boundary factors gives a weighted complete-intersection description of the pair image. Pullback preserves the Hilbert lower bound, and an additional field generator can double its leading term.
- **claim-quartic-surface-hilbert** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-quartic-surface)): Let $Y$ be a geometrically irreducible surface defined over $\mathbb Q$ in the rational coordinates of the arithmetic boxes. Suppose that it meets the open $a_1a_2a_3a_4\ne0$, satisfies $$a_i^2b_i=n-\theta_i\qquad(1\le i\le4),$$ and that $n$ is nonconstant and algebraic over the field generated by $a_1,\ldots,a_4$. Give the two coordinate blocks positive rational weights $U,V$, respectively, and put $$D_0=\min(3U,2V,U+V).$$ Then $$\label{eq:quartic-surface-hilbert} H_Y(T)\ge \frac{T^2}{2} \min\left\{\frac{(2U+V)D_0}{U^2V^2},\frac3{U^2}\right\}-O(T).$$ For fixed weights and a bound on $\deg Y$, the error is uniform in the moving coordinate changes and defining equations.
  - Explanation: The degree of the first-block image separates the proof into higher-degree, quadric and plane cases. Field-degree gains and critical-value obstructions strengthen the general pair-image estimate.
- **claim-global-quintic-exceptional-set** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-quintic-discard)): There are finitely many polynomials $\Phi\in L[t]$ of degree five with distinct critical points $z_1=0,z_2=1,z_3,z_4$ satisfying $$\Phi(z_i)=\theta_i\qquad(1\le i\le4).$$ Let $\mathcal F_f$ be this finite family and put $$\mathcal E_f= \{n\in\mathbb Z:n=\Phi(z)\text{ for some } \Phi\in\mathcal F_f, z\in L\}.$$ Then, for $X\ge1$, $$\label{eq:quintic-discard-count} \#\bigl(\mathcal E_f\cap[-2X,2X]\bigr)\ll_f X^{1/5}.$$ Moreover, if $N(s)\in L[s]$ has degree five and distinct critical points $\tau_1,\ldots,\tau_4\in L$ with $N(\tau_i)=\theta_i$, every integer value $N(s)$ at $s\in L$ belongs to $\mathcal E_f$.
  - Explanation: Normalizing critical points makes the exceptional quintics a fixed finite family. Their rational-value locus is a curve inside a fixed fractional lattice, so their integer values form a globally sparse set.
- **claim-uniform-integral-rational-values** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-values)): Fix a positive integer $D$ and positive constants $A,B,C_0$. For every $\varepsilon>0$ and $X\ge2$, let $R\in\mathbb Q(s)$ be a nonpolynomial rational function whose reduced numerator and denominator have degrees at most $D$. Then $$\#\{s\in\mathbb Z: \|s\|\le C_0X^A,\ R(s)\in\mathbb Z, \ \|R(s)\|\le C_0X^B\} \ll_{D,A,B,C_0,\varepsilon}X^\varepsilon.$$ The constant is independent of the coefficients of $R$. Inputs at which $R$ has a pole are excluded from the set.
  - Explanation: Bounded integral input-output pairs reconstruct a polynomial-height rational representation. Its resultant restricts denominator values to divisors of a bounded integer, giving coefficient-uniform sparsity.
- **claim-arithmetic-curve-alternatives** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-curve-alternatives)): Put $$g=(d-1)(k-1)+\mathbf 1_{d=4},\qquad \delta_1(U,V)=\max\{U/2,\min(U,V/g)\}.$$ Fix a degree bound $E$, and let $C$ be a geometrically irreducible curve of degree at most $E$, defined over $\mathbb Q$, in the arithmetic variety $a_i^k b_i=n-\theta_i$ in the coordinates just described. For every $\varepsilon>0$, either $$\|S\cap C\|\ll_{f,E,\varepsilon}X^\varepsilon,$$ or its filtered Hilbert dimension, with weight $U$ on $x'$ and weight $V$ on $y'$, satisfies $$\label{eq:curve-hilbert-bound} H_C(T)\ge \frac{T}{\delta_1(U,V)}-O_{f,E}(1).$$ The implied constants may also depend on a fixed compact subset of $(0,\infty)$ containing $U,V$. They are otherwise uniform in the weights and in the integral coordinate changes. In particular they are uniform for the weights arising from Proposition <a href="#prop:lattice-boxes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:lattice-boxes">17</a>.
  - Explanation: Curves either have few selected integral points or enough independent polynomial functions for affine counting. Nonpolynomial rational coordinates give the small-count case; polynomial line curves use the global exceptional-value removal in the quartic case.
- **claim-exact-parameter-choice-and-shift** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-parameter-certificate)): For each $d\in\{4,5,6,7,8\}$, the stopping index in <a href="#eq:parameter-stop" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameter-stop">[eq:parameter-stop]</a> is the one listed in Table <a href="#tab:parameter-certificate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tab:parameter-certificate">1</a>. For every integer $K_0\le z<z_d$, there are positive rational numbers $t_*,w$ such that, with $m=(d-1)w$, $$\begin{gathered} 0<m<1,\qquad \min(\eta_-,b_-)>m,\\ w^{d-1}H(t_*)> \left(\frac{(d+1)(\eta_++b_+t_*)}{d^2}\right)^d,\\ w^{d-2}\min\{H_a(t_*),H_b(t_*)\}> \left(\frac{\eta_++b_+t_*}{d-1}\right)^{d-1}. \end{gathered} \label{eq:parameter-cut-inequalities}$$ In particular, the cut conditions <a href="#eq:cut-conditions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cut-conditions">[eq:cut-conditions]</a> hold. For $$u=\frac{\eta_+-m}{d},\qquad v=\frac{b_+-m}{d},$$ these choices also satisfy $$\frac3{20}\le\frac vu\le5,\qquad m+\sum_{h=1}^dE_h(u,v)<\frac{999}{1000}, \qquad m+2u<\frac{999}{1000}\quad(\text{mode }\mathsf Q). \label{eq:parameter-saving}$$ Moreover, for either fixed mode, every $U,V>0$ with $3/20\le V/U\le5$ satisfies $$\delta_h(U+s,V+s)\le\delta_h(U,V)+s \quad(s\ge0,\ 1\le h\le d). \label{eq:parameter-shift}$$
  - Explanation: Exact rational choices satisfy the determinant inequalities and leave a strict summation margin on every parameter interval. A separate directional derivative argument bounds the effect of increasing both coordinate weights.
- **claim-cyclotomic-squarefree-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-cyclotomic-density)): Put $$c_8=\prod_{p\equiv1\pmod8}\left(1-\frac4{p^2}\right).$$ Then $c_8>3/4$, and $$\#\{1\le n\le X:n^4+1\text{ is squarefree}\}=c_8X+o(X).$$ Consequently, for every sufficiently large real $N$, $$\#\{n\in\mathbb Z:N\le n\le2N,\ n^4+1\text{ is squarefree}\} \ge N/2.$$
  - Explanation: The cyclotomic quartic has explicitly computable local root counts. They give a positive density exceeding the stated lower bound and a corresponding eventual dyadic proportion.
- **claim-cyclotomic-central-prime-saving** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-cyclotomic-central)): With $\epsilon_0=10^{-6}$, there exists $\delta_0>0$ such that $$\#\{n\in\mathbb Z:N\le n\le2N,\ p^2\mid n^4+1\text{ for some prime } N^{1-\epsilon_0}\le p\le N^{3/2+\epsilon_0}\} \ll N^{1-\delta_0}.$$
  - Explanation: An enlarged quartic parameter cell extends the power-saving argument slightly below the input scale. The arithmetic multiplicity condition still holds because the prime square exceeds the interval length.
- **claim-uniform-cyclotomic-pell-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cyclotomic-pell)): Uniformly in the positive integer $D$, the number of positive integer pairs $(n,q)$ with $N\le n\le2N$ and $n^4+1=Dq^2$ is $O(1+\log N)$. Consequently, for every fixed $\epsilon>0$, the number of inputs in this interval with $p^2\mid n^4+1$ for some prime $p>N^{3/2+\epsilon}$ is $O(N^{1-2\epsilon}(1+\log N))$.
  - Explanation: Solutions of the negative Pell equation have a fixed multiplicative separation independent of the coefficient. Summing this uniform bound over possible cofactors controls the upper prime range.

        "exact_quote": "then $$S_{\\mathbf F,k}(N)=c_{\\mathbf F,k}N+o_{\\mathbf F,k}(N),\\qquad\n c_{\\mathbf F,k}=\\prod_p\\left(1-\\frac{\\#\\Omega_p}{p^k}\\right)>0.$$",

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-density-content-2",

          "source_id": "PM-020.1"

      "exact_statement": "Fix an integer $k\\ge2$. Let $F\\in\\mathbb Z[x]\\setminus\\{0\\}$ be separable over $\\mathbb Q$, with every nonconstant irreducible factor of degree at most $k+2$. If $\\rho_F(p^k)<p^k$ for every prime $p$, then $$S_{F,k}(N)=c_{F,k}N+o_{F,k}(N),\\qquad\n c_{F,k}=\\prod_p\\left(1-\\frac{\\rho_F(p^k)}{p^k}\\right)>0.$$ More generally, let $\\mathbf F=(F_1,\\ldots,F_r)$, with $r\\ge1$, be a fixed family of nonzero integer polynomials, each separable over $\\mathbb Q$ with all nonconstant irreducible factors of degree at most $k+2$. Factors may be shared by different $F_i$. Put $$\\Omega_p=\\{a\\in\\mathbb Z/p^k\\mathbb Z:p^k\\mid F_i(a)\\text{ for at least one }i\\}.$$ Let $S_{\\mathbf F,k}(N)$ count the integers $1\\le n\\le N$ for which every $F_i(n)$ is $k$-power-free. If $\\#\\Omega_p<p^k$ for every prime $p$, then $$S_{\\mathbf F,k}(N)=c_{\\mathbf F,k}N+o_{\\mathbf F,k}(N),\\qquad\n c_{\\mathbf F,k}=\\prod_p\\left(1-\\frac{\\#\\Omega_p}{p^k}\\right)>0.$$",

      "exact_statement": "Fix $0<w<1$ and an integer $M\\asymp X^w$. Partition each of the $s$- and $t$-cubes into grid boxes of side $M^{-1}$. For the selected points in any fixed arithmetic and coordinate chart, the following hold uniformly.*\n\n1.  *The vector $t$ is locally an analytic function of $(s,z)$. Conversely, $s$ is locally given by boundedly many analytic branches in $(t,z)$.*\n\n2.  *The branches needed at a counted point extend to a neighborhood of fixed radius containing the corresponding grid base with last coordinate $z=0$. Derivatives of every fixed order are uniformly bounded there.*\n\n3.  *Every relevant box of either type has $O_f(1)$ partner boxes of the other type.         "exact_quote": "Then $D>0$, and the weighted filtered Hilbert function of the pair image satisfies $$H_{Y_{\\mathrm{pair}}}(T)=\n \\frac{(kU+V)^{h-1}D}{U^hV^h}\\frac{T^h}{h!}\n +O(T^{h-1}+1).\n \\label{eq:pair-hilbert}$$",

      "exact_statement": "For each nonzero coefficient polynomial $f_I$, define $$\\nu_{i,I}=\\operatorname{ord}_{n-\\theta_i}f_I,\\qquad\n e_i=\\min_I(I_i+k\\nu_{i,I}),$$ and put $$D=\\max_I\\{U|I|+(kU+V)\\deg f_I\\}-U\\sum_i e_i.\n \\label{eq:extracted-weight}$$ Then $D>0$, and the weighted filtered Hilbert function of the pair image satisfies $$H_{Y_{\\mathrm{pair}}}(T)=\n \\frac{(kU+V)^{h-1}D}{U^hV^h}\\frac{T^h}{h!}\n +O(T^{h-1}+1).\n \\label{eq:pair-hilbert}$$ This is a lower bound for $H_Y(T)$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Power-free integer** ([source](#eq:local-admissibility)): For an integer $k\ge2$, an integer $a$ is *$k$-power-free* if no prime power $p^k$ divides $a$.
- **Root count and power-free counting function** ([source](#eq:local-admissibility)): Given $f\in\mathbb Z[x]$, write $$\rho_f(q)=\#\{a\in\mathbb Z/q\mathbb Z:f(a)\equiv0\pmod q\},\qquad S_{f,k}(X)=\#\{1\le n\le X:f(n)\text{ is $k$-power-free}\}.$$
- **Local admissibility** ([source](#eq:local-admissibility)): The necessary local condition is $$\rho_f(p^k)<p^k\quad\text{for every prime }p. \label{eq:local-admissibility}$$
- **Weighted filtered Hilbert dimension** ([source](#sec:affine-counting)): For an affine algebraic set $A\subseteq\mathbb C^N$, define $$H_A(T)=\dim_{\mathbb C}\operatorname{span} \{z^\alpha\|_A:\omega\cdot\alpha\le T\}.$$
- **Smooth affine patch** ([source](#pm-h-9)): A *patch* will mean a triple $(A,G,\Delta)$, where $A$ is an irreducible $\mathbb Q$-defined variety of dimension $h$, the list $G$ consists of $N-h$ integer polynomials vanishing on $A$, and $\Delta$ is a square Jacobian minor in $N-h$ chosen coordinate columns.
- **Ordered local monomial degree sum** ([source](#eq:affine-local-orders)): For $h\ge1$, let $s_h(L)$ be the sum of the first $L$ total degrees of monomials in $h$ variables, ordered by degree and counted with their monomial multiplicities.
- **Deterministic evaluation kernel** ([source](#eq:affine-evaluation-kernel)): Given its parent patch at step $i$, let $$\label{eq:affine-evaluation-kernel} \mathcal K_i= \left\{F\in\mathbb Q[z_1,\ldots,z_N]_{\le D_i}: F(z')=0\text{ for all }z'\in S\cap A_{i-1},\ z'\equiv z\pmod{Q_i} \right\}.$$
- **Regular selected point** ([source](#pm-h-12)): Call $z\in S$ *regular* if in each block at most a fraction $\xi$ of its primes divide any of the full-path minors at $z$.
- **Common paired-lattice excess** ([source](#eq:lattice-J-bound)): Define the excess $$J=\max\{1,U_*/X^u,V_*/X^v\}.$$
- **Weighted pair threshold** ([source](#eq:parameter-thresholds)): For $U,V,D>0$ and $h\ge2$, write $$\Psi_h(U,V;D) =\frac{UV}{\bigl((kU+V)^{h-1}D\bigr)^{1/h}}, \qquad g=(d-1)(k-1)+\mathbf1_{d=4}.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-controlled-patches** [claim](#stmt-lem-affine-patches) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-affine-patches): Descend nonrational components, reconstruct bounded-height equations from evaluations, and recurse on proper intersections and vanishing minors. -> Deterministic bounded patch cover with polynomial-height evaluated minors.
- **step-residue-determinants** [claim](#stmt-lem-affine-residue-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-affine-residue-cut): Compare prime-adic divisibility of integral evaluation determinants with their weighted archimedean upper bounds. -> Proper bounded-degree polynomial cut of the full simultaneous residue class.
- **step-adaptive-deletion** [claim](#stmt-lem-affine-deletion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-affine-deletion): Encode the complete evaluation-rank path and delete a uniformly sampled prime, separating rank changes from divisibility of fixed deleted-path minors. -> Uniform expected bound on the fraction of bad primes.
- **step-affine-counting-tree** [claim](#stmt-thm-affine-counting) / [proof](#pm-h-12): Choose regular points by averaging, use cumulative good products for successive proper cuts, and telescope branching exponents. -> Uniform integer-point bound for moving affine sets.
- **step-balanced-factorization** [claim](#stmt-lem-balanced-factorization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-balanced-factorization): Use simple degree-one prime ideals, fixed ideal-class corrections and the logarithmic unit lattice to produce balanced integral factors. -> Selected balanced representations with bounded first-factor multiplicity.
- **step-paired-charts** [claim](#stmt-lem-paired-boxes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-paired-boxes): Normalize embedding coordinates and use rational operations and local root branches with denominators separated from zero. -> Uniform analytic branches and boundedly many partner boxes.
- **step-bicone-ring** [claim](#stmt-lem-bicone) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bicone): Count boundary strata, apply the complete-intersection criterion, and extract the leading term of the Hilbert series. -> Prime bicone and explicit bihomogeneous Hilbert polynomial.
- **step-archimedean-cuts** [claim](#stmt-prop-archimedean-cuts) / [proof](#pm-h-19): Force the first determinant to vanish, saturate the component equations, and apply a coefficient-uniform second determinant on pure components. -> Mixed complete-intersection sections or smaller-dimensional cut sets.
- **step-lattice-basis** [claim](#stmt-lem-lattice-basis) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lattice-basis): Induct using a shortest primitive vector and orthogonal projection, then use Cramer’s rule for coordinate bounds. -> Basis product bound and integral-coordinate control.
- **step-lattice-grouping** [claim](#stmt-prop-lattice-boxes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-lattice-boxes): Encode approximation boxes by triangular lattices, rebase integrally, and recover prime-grid bases from short residual vectors. -> Grouped box counts and transformed blockwise coordinate bounds.
- **step-quartic-fiber-deletion** [claim](#stmt-lem-quartic-fibers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quartic-fibers): Set all input-polynomial coefficients to zero to identify infinite fibers, bound their base dimension, and count exceptional first-block integers. -> Removal cost and algebraicity of the input over the first-block field.
- **step-pair-hilbert** [claim](#stmt-prop-weighted-hilbert) / [proof](#pm-h-27): Extract coordinate boundary factors, check the complete intersection at weighted infinity, and use independent field generators for an extension gain. -> Uniform weighted Hilbert leading coefficient and optional doubling.
- **step-quartic-surface-geometry** [claim](#stmt-prop-quartic-surface) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-quartic-surface): Separate the first-block image by degree; use projection field degrees for quadrics and critical-value contradictions for planes. -> Strengthened quartic surface Hilbert threshold.
- **step-global-quintics** [claim](#stmt-prop-quintic-discard) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-quintic-discard): Normalize critical points, prove the parameter locus has no positive-dimensional component, and count a rational-value curve in a fractional lattice. -> Fixed finite family and a globally sparse exceptional-value set.
- **step-rational-integral-values** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-values) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-values): Reconstruct a bounded-height representation from integral input-output pairs and restrict denominator values by its resultant. -> Coefficient-uniform sparse integral-value bound.
- **step-curve-dichotomy** [claim](#stmt-prop-curve-alternatives) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-curve-alternatives): Use independent function families for nonlinear projections; use rational-value sparsity or polynomial degree constraints for line images. -> Exceptional small-count alternative or uniform curve Hilbert bound.
- **step-parameter-certificate** [claim](#stmt-prop-parameter-certificate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-parameter-certificate): Search a finite rational ratio grid, round radical bounds strictly upward, verify every interval exactly, and bound directional derivatives for common shifts. -> Strict cut inequalities, savings and common-shift control.
- **step-direct-surface-tail** [claim](#stmt-prop-large-prime-tail) / [proof](#pm-h-41): Apply affine counting to the original polynomial surface with strictly positive upper weights, using rational-value alternatives on its curves. -> Power-saving tail estimate in the direct-surface range.
- **step-number-field-tail** [claim](#stmt-prop-large-prime-tail) / [proof](#pm-h-42): Apply affine counting in each transformed paired box, sum lattice groups using common-shift control, and charge global and fiber deletions. -> Power-saving tail estimate in the number-field range.
- **step-complete-prime-tail** [claim](#stmt-prop-large-prime-tail) / [proof](#pm-h-43): Take a common saving over the finite parameter ranges and sum dyadic prime intervals. -> Large-prime tail bound for the primitive polynomial.
- **step-sieve-transfer** [claim](#stmt-prop-sieve-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-sieve-transfer): Retain original content-prime factors, perform the finite congruence sieve, bound intermediate primes, and take the input and cutoff limits in order. -> Exact positive Euler-product density for the original polynomial.
- **step-main-density** [claim](#stmt-thm-density) / [proof](#pm-h-43): Apply sieve transfer to the sign-normalized primitive part after proving its large-prime tail. -> Low-degree power-free density with original local factors.
- **step-content-density-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-density-content) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-density-content): Compare the finite-sieve deficits of the original polynomial and its primitive part, then pass to the cutoff limit. -> Density restored across sign and content.
- **step-higher-degrees** [claim](#stmt-cor-all-degrees) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-all-degrees): Combine the low-degree theorem with the cited higher-degree range and restore original content factors. -> Power-free density in every stated degree.
- **step-separable-families** [claim](#stmt-cor-separable-products) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-density-content-2): Establish factorwise tails, exclude shared roots outside finitely many resultant primes, and sieve the joint forbidden residue sets. -> Separable-product and simultaneous-family densities.
- **step-cyclotomic-density** [claim](#stmt-cor-cyclotomic-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-cyclotomic-density): Compute roots using their multiplicative order, lift simple roots, and bound the Euler product by a convergent reciprocal-square tail. -> Explicit density and eventual dyadic lower proportion.
- **step-central-prime-extension** [claim](#stmt-prop-cyclotomic-central) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cyclotomic-central): Verify the enlarged quartic cell exactly and recheck bad-prime exclusion and root-class multiplicity below the input scale. -> Power saving throughout the central prime band.
- **step-cyclotomic-pell** [claim](#stmt-lem-cyclotomic-pell) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cyclotomic-pell): Associate norm-negative units to solutions, prove coefficient-independent multiplicative spacing, and sum over possible integer cofactors. -> Uniform logarithmic solution count and upper-prime bound.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-strict-upward-rounding**: The finite certificate rounds radical thresholds strictly upwards, including when a root already lies on the grid.
- **invariant-enlarged-cell-cuts**: The enlarged first quartic cell satisfies both strict determinant cut inequalities and the positive-weight and ratio conditions.
- **invariant-cumulative-maxima**: Dimension thresholds must be accumulated through prefix maxima in decreasing dimension order before computing the counting margin.
- **invariant-positive-enlarged-cell-margins**: The enlarged cell leaves positive margins after the lattice shift and the infinite-fiber deletion costs.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-certificate-as-density-proof**: Treat successful numerical checks as a certificate of the entire density theorem. -> The numerical test does not establish the arithmetic factorization, geometric estimates, large-prime tail, or limiting sieve argument.
- **anti-infer-arithmetic-from-parameters**: Extend the prime range solely because the enlarged numerical cell passes. -> Parameter inequalities alone do not justify the selected-point multiplicities and arithmetic factorization.
- **anti-round-root-nonstrictly**: Accept a grid point equal to a radical threshold. -> Equality eliminates the strict margin used to force determinant vanishing.
- **anti-claim-uniform-density-error**: Use the density asymptotic as a uniform finite sampling guarantee over changing polynomials. -> The source does not assert uniformity of the density error as the polynomial varies.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The source supplies a finite exact calculation for the enlarged first quartic cell and a strict upward-rounding procedure. This implementation covers that numerical subresult and boundary behavior of rounding. It does not certify the asymptotic density theorem or the full parameter table. The source states: "The finite calculation below concerns these numerical inequalities; the geometric estimates supplying the counting thresholds were proved in the preceding sections."

```python
# INPUTS: One JSON object selecting cell or upper; upper supplies a rational numerator, denominator, and exponent.
# PRECONDITIONS: The upper argument lies in the half-open unit interval; its exponent is a positive integer.
# OUTPUTS: Exact grid index, or enlarged-cell rounded thresholds, margins, and condition flags as JSON.
# TIME: Logarithmic grid search with exact arithmetic; the cell performs a fixed finite collection of searches.
# SPACE: A fixed collection of rational values, with storage determined by integer operand sizes.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers and fractions; no floating-point evaluation.
# FAILURE: Invalid requests raise an exception; failed cell conditions are reported as false flags.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction as F

G = 100000

def upper(x, e):
    if not 0 <= x < 1 or type(e) is not int or e < 1:
        raise ValueError('invalid upper request')
    l, r = 0, G
    while r > l + 1:
        middle = (l + r) // 2
        if F(middle, G)**e > x:
            r = middle
        else:
            l = middle
    return F(r, G)

def cell():
    epsilon = F(1, 10**6)
    etaL, etaH = 1-epsilon, F(3001, 3000)
    bL, bH = 4-2*etaH, 2+2*epsilon
    t, w = F(1, 2), F(24809, 100000)
    m = 3*w
    u, v = (etaH-m)/4, (bH-m)/4
    def delta(h, D):
        return upper((u*v)**h/((2*u+v)**(h-1)*D), h)
    P = [delta(4, u+v), delta(3, v),
         max(delta(2, min(3*u, 2*v, u+v)), upper(u*u/3, 2)),
         max(u/2, min(u, v/4))]
    gap = 1-m-sum(max(P[:i]) for i in range(1, 5))
    tau = F(1, 50000)
    return {
        'w': str(w),
        'thresholds': [str(p) for p in P],
        'gap_after_shift': str(gap-4*tau),
        'fiber_gap': str(1-m-2*u-2*tau),
        'conditions': {
            'epsilon': 0 < epsilon < F(1, 2),
            'box': 0 < m < 1 and m < min(etaL, bL),
            'first_cut': w**3*10 > (F(5, 16)*(etaH+bH*t))**4,
            'second_cut': w**2*5 > ((etaH+bH*t)/3)**3,
            'weights': min(u, v) > 0 and F(3, 20) <= v/u <= 5,
            'counting_margin': gap-4*tau > 0,
            'fiber_margin': 1-m-2*u-2*tau > 0
        }
    }

def main():
    request = json.load(sys.stdin)
    if request.get('mode') == 'cell':
        if set(request) != {'mode'}:
            raise ValueError('unexpected cell fields')
        result = cell()
    elif request.get('mode') == 'upper':
        if set(request) != {'mode', 'numerator', 'denominator', 'exponent'}:
            raise ValueError('unexpected upper fields')
        n, d, e = request['numerator'], request['denominator'], request['exponent']
        if type(n) is not int or type(d) is not int or d <= 0:
            raise ValueError('invalid rational input')
        result = {'grid_index': int(upper(F(n, d), e)*G)}
    else:
        raise ValueError('unknown mode')
    print(json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True))

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The supplied exact parameter certificate gives a concrete implementation: construct rational parameter candidates, select the prescribed candidate, round radical thresholds strictly upwards, and assert the cut and saving inequalities. This supports reproducible mathematical verification software. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paired boxes organize selected arithmetic points through analytic coordinate maps. They supply no evolution rule, collision model, or sampling contract for a game or simulation. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A mathematical checking tool can implement the supplied rational certificate and report which prescribed parameter inequalities hold. The construction checks the entire finite interval collection and the endpoint derivative inequalities, making it suitable for reproducible certificate checking. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The source supplies a finite algorithm for choosing rational determinant parameters and conservatively bounding radical thresholds. Its binary search for a strict upper grid bound and its exhaustive interval checks are concrete algorithmic results within the prescribed parameter family. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The adaptive proof records polynomial evaluation kernels and ranks, but it does not specify a reusable storage representation or data structure operation contract. The complete kernel is needed for a mathematical equality argument rather than a claimed implementation performance result. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The lattice basis result bounds mathematical coordinates through covolume and shortest length. It supplies no accelerator architecture, parallel execution construction, or physical resource estimate. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The density argument identifies a limiting Euler product by successive limits. It provides neither an individual value certificate nor a finite security parameter contract for cryptographic generation. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The analytic matrix estimate derives approximate rank from a common nonzero holomorphic relation on shrinking arithmetic charts. It does not construct a learned model, establish generalization, or justify low rank for arbitrary training data. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The parameter prescription performs a concrete finite optimisation: enumerate the prescribed rational bidegree ratios, minimize the stated objective with the prescribed tie breaking, and check the resulting determinant and counting margins. This applies to the paper's parameter selection problem. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Deterministic patch selection ensures identical mathematical constructions for identical inputs. The paper gives no message protocol, failure model, replication construction, or agreement guarantee for distributed processes. |
| other (Exact computational verification of arithmetic parameter inequalities) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The finite certificate explicitly checks the numerical inequalities needed by the arithmetic geometry argument. A direct construction reproduces the rational parameter prescription, the conservative threshold maxima, the stopping indices, and the enlarged quartic cell checks. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-irreducible-degree-exponent-regime** (hypothesis): The main density theorem requires rational irreducibility and the stated relation between degree and excluded power. Higher-degree power-freeness does not establish squarefreeness. Blocked use: ['Claiming squarefree values for every higher-degree polynomial.', 'Applying the irreducible theorem directly to an arbitrary reducible polynomial.']
- **limit-local-obstructions** (hypothesis): Positive density requires the absence of a fixed prime power divisor at every prime. Checking a finite selection of local factors alone does not establish this universal hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Claiming positive density when a prime power divides every polynomial value.', 'Treating sampled local admissibility checks as a proof of the complete local condition.']
- **limit-fixed-polynomial-error** (formal_scope): The density concerns positive integer inputs for a fixed polynomial. The error is not asserted to be uniform over changing coefficient families. Blocked use: ['Using the density error uniformly for polynomials whose coefficients vary with the input scale.', 'Replacing a fixed-polynomial theorem by an averaged coefficient-family statement.']
- **limit-asymptotic-finite-window** (unknown): An explicit finite input threshold and finite-window density error are ABSENT from the stated density conclusion. The eventual dyadic lower proportion does not certify an arbitrary prescribed window. Blocked use: ['Guaranteeing squarefree output in a specified small input window from the limiting density.', 'Assigning a certified finite sampling success probability from the Euler product alone.']
- **limit-zero-and-negative-values** (edge_case): Power-freeness includes negative integers and excludes zero. Polynomial zeros must therefore be excluded when extending counting constructions to separable polynomials. Blocked use: ['Counting zero as power-free.', 'Rejecting negative values solely because of their sign.']
- **limit-separable-factor-degrees** (hypothesis): The product extension requires separability and the stated bound on every nonconstant irreducible factor. It does not authorize arbitrary repeated factors. Blocked use: ['Applying the separable-product density to a polynomial with repeated nonconstant irreducible factors.', 'Ignoring an irreducible factor whose degree exceeds the allowed factor-degree regime.']
- **limit-simultaneous-joint-local-set** (formal_scope): Simultaneous power-freeness is imposed separately on each polynomial and uses the joint forbidden residue set. Individual densities cannot generally be multiplied, and simultaneous power-freeness does not imply power-freeness of the product. Blocked use: ['Multiplying individual Euler products to obtain the simultaneous density.', 'Replacing separate power-free conditions by a power-free condition on the full product.']
- **limit-content-local-factors** (hypothesis): Sign normalization preserves divisibility, but removing content can change local factors. The finite sieve must retain the original polynomial's content-prime conditions. Blocked use: ["Substituting the primitive polynomial's Euler product for the original polynomial's product.", 'Separating content-prime conditions without restoring their exact valuation thresholds.']
- **limit-tail-normalization** (hypothesis): The large-prime proposition is stated for the primitive positive-leading polynomial in its specified degree and power regime. Transfer to a general polynomial requires the separate normalization and local-factor argument. Blocked use: ['Applying the primitive tail statement to an unnormalized polynomial without establishing the transfer.', 'Requiring local admissibility as an additional hypothesis of the large-prime tail itself.']
- **limit-sieve-limit-order** (formal_scope): The finite-prime cutoff is held fixed while the input scale grows, and only afterwards tends to infinity. This argument supplies the density without asserting a power-saving density error. Blocked use: ['Allowing the sieve cutoff to grow arbitrarily with the input scale and retaining the same error argument.', 'Transferring the tail power saving directly to a power-saving error for the density.']
- **limit-overlapping-congruences** (formal_scope): Additional fixed congruence restrictions must be combined using their least common multiple. Overlapping conditions do not supply independent survival probabilities. Blocked use: ['Multiplying survival fractions for overlapping congruence conditions.', 'Discarding original content conditions when imposing arithmetic progression restrictions.']
- **limit-affine-all-subvarieties** (hypothesis): Affine counting requires uniform exceptional-count or Hilbert alternatives on every relevant bounded-degree subvariety. Verifying only the initial components is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Using the affine count after checking Hilbert bounds only on the initial variety.', 'Allowing the alternative constants to depend without control on moving equations or selected sets.']
- **limit-patch-locality-and-good-primes** (hypothesis): Patch equations supply local coordinates only where the chosen minor is nonzero. The residue determinant requires primes avoiding the evaluated minor and a sufficiently large product. Blocked use: ['Assuming a patch globally presents its variety as a complete intersection.', 'Using primes dividing the evaluated minor in the smooth local determinant argument.']
- **limit-adaptive-full-kernels** (hypothesis): Adaptive deletion depends on deterministic full paths and entire vanishing polynomial spaces. A single selected equation does not preserve the rank-stability implication. Blocked use: ['Replacing a full evaluation kernel by one vanishing polynomial.', 'Selecting patches using extra prime information beyond the input variety and selected set.']
- **limit-deletion-sampling-regime** (hypothesis): The deletion estimate uses disjoint growing prime pools and the prescribed consecutive-size uniform sampling. It bounds an expected bad-prime fraction rather than declaring every block good. Blocked use: ['Applying the expected bound to arbitrarily chosen deterministic blocks.', 'Replacing the prescribed random block-size law without proving a new deletion estimate.']
- **limit-balanced-selected-representations** (formal_scope): Arithmetic multiplicity bounds concern selected representations. Norm factorization alone does not balance conjugates, and counting all possible generators does not inherit the selected-point bounds. Blocked use: ['Claiming balanced embedding sizes directly from the norm equation.', 'Counting every unit multiple as a separate selected input.']
- **limit-paired-charts-local-branches** (hypothesis): Partner-box bounds use local analytic branches with uniformly controlled neighborhoods and derivatives. They do not provide a single global root branch. Blocked use: ['Choosing a global root on the entire annulus without a separate justification.', 'Applying partner-box counts to unrelated coordinate maps lacking the stated derivative bounds.']
- **limit-pure-component-second-cut** (hypothesis): Pure components require their additional determinant cut. Its uniformity comes from exact polynomial evaluation dependence without dividing by a potentially small coefficient. Blocked use: ['Treating every first-cut component as mixed.', 'Solving a moving pure equation by division and assuming coefficient-uniform analytic bounds.']
- **limit-prime-grid-and-blockwise-weights** (hypothesis): The lattice box-count argument uses a prime approximation grid and integral unimodular rebasing. Filtration preservation within a coordinate block does not by itself bound the numerical sizes of points. Blocked use: ['Dropping grid primality from the lattice box-count proof because paired analytic boxes allow a composite grid.', 'Using filtration preservation as a substitute for transformed integral-coordinate bounds.']
- **limit-hilbert-open-and-field-degree** (hypothesis): The pair-image construction requires positive rational block weights, a nonempty nonzero-coordinate open, and a nonconstant input function. Doubling the leading coefficient requires the stated field extension degree. Blocked use: ['Applying the pair-image formula to a component supported entirely on a coordinate boundary.', 'Doubling a Hilbert coefficient without establishing the field extension degree.']
- **limit-quartic-fiber-removal** (hypothesis): The stronger quartic surface estimate requires algebraicity of the input over the first-coordinate field. Infinite fibers must be removed and their cost accounted for before invoking this property on remaining subvarieties. Blocked use: ['Applying the strengthened surface bound before establishing input algebraicity.', 'Discarding infinite fibers without charging their selected-point deletion cost.']
- **limit-global-quintic-discard** (formal_scope): The improved quartic curve threshold uses removal of the fixed global quintic-value set before selecting curves. Its cost is charged globally rather than repeatedly per box. Blocked use: ['Keeping the quintic equality curves while using the improved quartic threshold.', 'Multiplying the global exceptional-value cost by the number of curves or boxes.']
- **limit-rational-value-nonpolynomial-domain** (hypothesis): The sparse integral-value estimate requires a nonpolynomial rational function of bounded reduced degrees, bounded integral inputs and outputs, and exclusion of poles. Blocked use: ['Applying rational-value sparsity to polynomial functions.', 'Counting poles as valid integral outputs.', 'Removing the output-size restriction while claiming coefficient-uniform sparsity.']
- **limit-exact-parameter-algorithm** (formal_scope): Parameter selection minimizes over the prescribed finite rational grid, retains strict upward radical rounding, and accumulates conservative thresholds through cumulative maxima. The finite search does not establish a global optimum over all ratios. Blocked use: ['Claiming continuous global optimality from finite candidate minimization.', 'Accepting equality when rounding a radical threshold.', 'Replacing cumulative maxima by an ordinary threshold sum.']
- **limit-numerical-certificate-coverage** (formal_scope): Finite arithmetic checks establish the numerical inequalities they cover. They do not certify the geometric prerequisites, the complete parameter family when only the enlarged cell is tested, or the asymptotic density theorem. Blocked use: ['Reporting a successful enlarged-cell check as verification of the complete parameter table.', 'Treating finite parameter checks as a proof of the full density theorem.']
- **limit-terminal-positive-upper-weights** (edge_case): The direct surface argument retains strictly positive upper weights even when the nominal cofactor exponent vanishes. Endpoint coverage and the fixed-coefficient overshoot require the separate upper-weight construction. Blocked use: ['Using a zero coordinate weight in the positive-weight affine counting theorem.', 'Leaving a gap between the number-field parameter intervals and the terminal direct-surface range.']
- **limit-central-band-arithmetic-checks** (hypothesis): Extending the cyclotomic prime band below the input scale requires independent arithmetic checks. Numerical margins alone do not establish bad-prime exclusion or selected-point multiplicity. Blocked use: ['Extending the prime band solely because the enlarged parameter cell passes.', 'Assuming the original prime-size hypothesis may be removed without checking its uses.']
- **limit-cyclotomic-pell-specialization** (formal_scope): The Pell estimate concerns the displayed cyclotomic quartic equation with a positive integer cofactor and positive integer pairs. Its uniformity does not extend the equation to arbitrary quartic polynomials. Blocked use: ['Using the same Pell count for a general quartic norm equation without a new argument.', 'Treating square cofactors as a source of positive solutions.']
- **limit-analytic-approximate-rank** (approximation): The analytic relation estimate requires a common relation for each evaluation matrix and a nonzero limiting germ. ### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 020 (`lean/docs/020.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Squarefree values of quartics and power-free values of polynomials](../../preprints/Squarefree-values-of-quartics-and-power-free-values-of-polynomials-September-24-2026/manuscript.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result proves positive-density power-free values for every integer polynomial $f$ irreducible over $\mathbb Q$ of degree $d\ge4$. Put $k=d-2$ and assume no prime $k$th power divides every value of $f$. Then the number of positive integers $n\le X$ for which $f(n)$ is $k$-free is $c_fX+o_f(X)$, where $c_f>0$ is the convergent product of local factors. Negative values are allowed and zero is excluded. No monicity, primitivity, or coefficient-height restriction is imposed.

Comparator source `PowerFreeValues.lean` (1380 bytes, sha256 `5e42de8cf6611fb73cab28849edb8b473ab16a047c1fc29ec042463e87a1f2f1`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI



open Filter Asymptotics
open scoped Topology

namespace QuarticPowerFree

def PowerFree (k : ℕ) (a : ℤ) : Prop :=
  ∀ p : ℕ, p.Prime → ¬ (p : ℤ) ^ k ∣ a

def rho (f : Polynomial ℤ) (q : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  ((Finset.range q).filter fun a : ℕ => (q : ℤ) ∣ f.eval (a : ℤ)).card

noncomputable def count (f : Polynomial ℤ) (k : ℕ) (X : ℝ) : ℕ := by
  classical
  exact ((Finset.Icc 1 ⌊X⌋₊).filter fun n : ℕ => PowerFree k (f.eval (n : ℤ))).card

def LocallyAdmissible (f : Polynomial ℤ) (k : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ p : ℕ, p.Prime → rho f (p ^ k) < p ^ k

noncomputable def localFactor (f : Polynomial ℤ) (k : ℕ) (p : Nat.Primes) : ℝ :=
  1 - (rho f (p.val ^ k) : ℝ) / (p.val : ℝ) ^ k

noncomputable def densityConstant (f : Polynomial ℤ) (k : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  ∏' p : Nat.Primes, localFactor f k p

def DensityStatement (f : Polynomial ℤ) (k : ℕ) : Prop :=
  Multipliable (localFactor f k) ∧
  0 < densityConstant f k ∧
  (fun X : ℝ => (count f k X : ℝ) - densityConstant f k * X) =o[atTop]
    (fun X : ℝ => X)

theorem allDegrees (f : Polynomial ℤ)
    (hirr : Irreducible (f.map (Int.castRingHom ℚ)))
    (hdeglo : 4 ≤ f.natDegree)
    (hlocal : LocallyAdmissible f (f.natDegree - 2)) :
    DensityStatement f (f.natDegree - 2) := by
  sorry

end QuarticPowerFree

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every irreducible integer quartic with no fixed prime-square divisor takes squarefree values with the predicted positive Euler-product density. More generally, we obtain the corresponding $`(d-2)`$-power-free density in degrees $`4\le d\le8`$. The proof combines number-field factorization, determinant estimates with adaptive auxiliary primes, and explicit low-degree geometry. Together with Browning's theorem for higher degrees, this gives the $`(d-2)`$-power-free density for every <i>d</i> ≥ 4.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

<a id="eq:local-admissibility"></a>
For an integer $k\ge2$, an integer $a$ is *$k$-power-free* if no prime power $p^k$ divides $a$. This convention includes negative integers and excludes zero. Given $f\in\mathbb Z[x]$, write $$\rho_f(q)=\#\{a\in\mathbb Z/q\mathbb Z:f(a)\equiv0\pmod q\},\qquad
 S_{f,k}(X)=\#\{1\le n\le X:f(n)\text{ is $k$-power-free}\}.$$ The necessary local condition is $$\rho_f(p^k)<p^k\quad\text{for every prime }p.
 \label{eq:local-admissibility}$$ It says that no prime $k$th power divides every value of $f$. The natural candidate for the density is the product of the proportions that survive these separate congruence conditions.

<a id="stmt-thm-density"></a>
<div id="thm:density" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $f\in\mathbb Z[x]$ be irreducible over $\mathbb Q$, of degree $d$ with $4\le d\le8$, and put $k=d-2$. If <a href="#eq:local-admissibility" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-admissibility">[eq:local-admissibility]</a> holds, then $$S_{f,k}(X)=c_{f,k}X+o_f(X),\qquad
 c_{f,k}=\prod_p\left(1-\frac{\rho_f(p^k)}{p^k}\right)>0.$$ No primitivity or sign condition on the leading coefficient is required.*

</div>

For quartics this is a squarefree-value density theorem. In degrees $5,6,7,8$, the respective excluded powers are $3,4,5,6$. The statement concerns positive integer inputs and imposes no bound on the height of the fixed polynomial’s coefficients. The error term is not asserted to be uniform as $f$ varies.

In particular, the quartics $x^4+2$ and $x^4+1$ have positive squarefree-value density. The first is Eisenstein at $2$; the second is the eighth cyclotomic polynomial. For $x^4+2$, its value at zero excludes a fixed odd prime square, and its value at one excludes a fixed square of $2$. The value at zero of $x^4+1$ is one, so its local condition is immediate.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Earlier results and the higher-degree consequence

The power-free-value problem has a long history. Ricci obtained the asymptotic in the range $k\ge d$ (Ricci 1933) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Ricci1933](#ref-Ricci1933)]. For $d\ge3$, Erdős proved infinitude for exponent $d-1$, and Hooley obtained the corresponding asymptotic (Erdős 1953; Hooley 1967) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Erdos1953](#ref-Erdos1953), [Hooley1967](#ref-Hooley1967)]; see also the historical discussion in (Heath-Brown 2013, Introduction) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[HeathBrown2013Powerfree](#ref-HeathBrown2013Powerfree)]. The cubic squarefree case therefore belongs to this classical range. Already in 1953, Erdős singled out the unresolved squarefreeness of $n^4+2$ (Erdős 1953, 425) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Erdos1953](#ref-Erdos1953)]. In his 1965 survey he returned to this example when describing the obstacle at exponent $d-2$ (Erdős 1965, sec. 6, p. 219) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Erdos1965](#ref-Erdos1965)].

Nair obtained the asymptotic for $k\ge(\sqrt2-\tfrac12)d$ (Nair 1976) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Nair1976](#ref-Nair1976)], with further developments by Nair and Huxley–Nair (Nair 1979; Huxley and Nair 1980) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Nair1979](#ref-Nair1979), [HuxleyNair1980](#ref-HuxleyNair1980)]. The 1976 bound reaches $k=d-2$ for $d\ge24$. Heath-Brown’s affine determinant method gave the range $k\ge(3d+2)/4$ (Heath-Brown 2006, Theorem 16) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[HeathBrown2006Counting](#ref-HeathBrown2006Counting)], reaching $k=d-2$ for $d\ge10$. Combining that method with Salberger’s global determinant estimates, Browning improved the range to $k\ge(3d+1)/4$ (T. D. Browning 2011) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Browning2011PowerFree](#ref-Browning2011PowerFree)], which reaches $k=d-2$ for $d\ge9$. We use the formulation and published reproof in Xiao (Xiao 2017, Theorem 9.1 and Section 9) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Xiao2017PowerFree](#ref-Xiao2017PowerFree)]: the input interval is $1\le n\le X$ and the constant is the Euler product over all residue classes modulo $p^k$.

The shape of the polynomial can permit stronger estimates. Heath-Brown proved an asymptotic with a power-saving error for irreducible binomials $x^d+c$ when $k\ge(5d+3)/9$ (Heath-Brown 2013, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[HeathBrown2013Powerfree](#ref-HeathBrown2013Powerfree)]. At $k=d-2$ this covers such binomials in degrees $d\ge6$, while imposing the special binomial form. Reuss obtained a power-saving error for the general degree-$d$ polynomial at exponent $d-1$ (Reuss 2015, Theorem 2) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Reuss2015PowerFree](#ref-Reuss2015PowerFree)]. The quartic theorem above concerns squarefree values at positive integer arguments for each fixed admissible polynomial.

Other important directions involve different hypotheses or quantifiers. Granville showed that the $abc$ conjecture yields the predicted squarefree-value density for separable integer polynomials with no fixed prime-square divisor (Granville 1998, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[granville1998abc](#ref-granville1998abc)]. Greaves and Helfgott studied squarefree values of binary forms; Helfgott’s Theorems 5.1–5.2 distinguish univariate cubics from homogeneous forms in two variables of degree at most six (Greaves 1992; Helfgott 2004) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Greaves1992](#ref-Greaves1992), [Helfgott2004](#ref-Helfgott2004)]. A density in two integer variables does not by itself give the density on the fixed line with second coordinate one. Recent results of Browning–Shparlinski and Sofos study the discrepancy from the predicted squarefree count on average over polynomial coefficient families (Tim D. Browning and Shparlinski 2024, Theorems 1.1–1.2) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[BrowningShparlinski2024](#ref-BrowningShparlinski2024)] <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Sofos2026](#ref-Sofos2026)], (Sofos 2026, Theorem 1.1); those averaged statements do not imply a theorem for every fixed polynomial.

Carella’s preprint claims squarefree-value asymptotics for both $n^4+1$ and $n^4+2$ (Carella 2023, Theorems 1.1–1.2) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[carella2023squarefree](#ref-carella2023squarefree)]. Zapata Ceballos–Jalalvand claim positive density for monic irreducible degree-$2q$ polynomials with squarefree fixed divisor whose root field contains a degree-$q$ Galois subfield, where $q$ is prime; this includes $n^4+1$ (Zapata Ceballos and Jalalvand 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[zapataceballosjalalvand2026squarefree](#ref-zapataceballosjalalvand2026squarefree)]. We do not use either claim as a theorem input. Our large-prime estimate and sieve transfer are proved independently below.

The new argument in this paper concerns degrees four through eight. For higher degrees, Browning’s theorem supplies a direct consequence.

<a id="stmt-cor-all-degrees"></a>
<div id="cor:all-degrees" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2**. *Let $f\in\mathbb Z[x]$ be irreducible over $\mathbb Q$ of degree $d\ge4$, and put $k=d-2$. If $f$ has no fixed prime $k$th-power divisor, then $$S_{f,k}(X)=c_{f,k}X+o_f(X),\qquad
 c_{f,k}=\prod_p\left(1-\frac{\rho_f(p^k)}{p^k}\right)>0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-all-degrees"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-all-degrees</summary>

*Proof.* For $4\le d\le8$ use Theorem <a href="#thm:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:density">1</a>. For $d\ge9$, $$d-2\ge\frac{3d+1}{4},$$ so Browning’s theorem, in the cited form of Xiao, applies to the primitive positive-leading part of $f$. Lemma <a href="#lem:density-content" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:density-content">7</a> transfers its density to $f$, retaining the original local factors. ◻

</details>

The factorwise large-prime estimates also give a fixed-product consequence. Here a nonzero polynomial is *separable over $\mathbb Q$* if each of its nonconstant irreducible factors occurs with multiplicity one.

<a id="stmt-cor-separable-products"></a>
<div id="cor:separable-products" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Separable products and simultaneous values). *Fix an integer $k\ge2$. Let $F\in\mathbb Z[x]\setminus\{0\}$ be separable over $\mathbb Q$, with every nonconstant irreducible factor of degree at most $k+2$. If $\rho_F(p^k)<p^k$ for every prime $p$, then $$S_{F,k}(N)=c_{F,k}N+o_{F,k}(N),\qquad
 c_{F,k}=\prod_p\left(1-\frac{\rho_F(p^k)}{p^k}\right)>0.$$ More generally, let $\mathbf F=(F_1,\ldots,F_r)$, with $r\ge1$, be a fixed family of nonzero integer polynomials, each separable over $\mathbb Q$ with all nonconstant irreducible factors of degree at most $k+2$. Factors may be shared by different $F_i$. Put $$\Omega_p=\{a\in\mathbb Z/p^k\mathbb Z:p^k\mid F_i(a)\text{ for at least one }i\}.$$ Let $S_{\mathbf F,k}(N)$ count the integers $1\le n\le N$ for which every $F_i(n)$ is $k$-power-free. If $\#\Omega_p<p^k$ for every prime $p$, then $$S_{\mathbf F,k}(N)=c_{\mathbf F,k}N+o_{\mathbf F,k}(N),\qquad
 c_{\mathbf F,k}=\prod_p\left(1-\frac{\#\Omega_p}{p^k}\right)>0.$$*

</div>

The first assertion requires the value of the entire product $F$ to be $k$-power-free. The second imposes power-freeness separately on the $F_i(n)$, not on their product, and uses the joint local condition $\Omega_p$ rather than a product of individual local factors. All polynomials, their number, and $k$ are fixed; no uniform or power-saving error is asserted here. The proof is at the end of Section <a href="#sec:sieve" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sieve">2</a>.

Our method combines number-field factorization with approximating determinants, following the framework developed by Reuss for power-free polynomial values (Reuss 2015, secs. 3–6) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Reuss2015PowerFree](#ref-Reuss2015PowerFree)]. Heath-Brown’s determinant arguments for squarefree values of $n^2+1$ and for power-free binomial values provide related precedents (Heath-Brown 2012, 2013) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[HeathBrown2012Squarefree](#ref-HeathBrown2012Squarefree), [HeathBrown2013Powerfree](#ref-HeathBrown2013Powerfree)]. The real determinant method originates in work of Bombieri–Pila; Heath-Brown developed the prime-adic and approximating forms used in this line of arguments, and Salberger developed their local Hilbert and global congruence aspects (Bombieri and Pila 1989; Heath-Brown 2002, 2009; Salberger 2007, 2023) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[BombieriPila1989](#ref-BombieriPila1989), [HeathBrown2002Density](#ref-HeathBrown2002Density), [HeathBrown2009Sums](#ref-HeathBrown2009Sums), [Salberger2007](#ref-Salberger2007), [Salberger2023](#ref-Salberger2023)]. The uniform affine counting theorem, the explicit weighted Hilbert calculations and the quartic surface and curve arguments needed here are proved below.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The large-prime problem

The finite-prime part of the problem follows from the Chinese remainder theorem. For primes outside a fixed finite set, separability and Hensel lifting give $\rho_f(p^k)\le d$. Consequently, for fixed large $Y$, primes $Y<p\le X$ remove at most $$O_f\bigl(XY^{1-k}+\pi(X)\bigr)$$ integers in $(X,2X]$. The remaining task is to control primes larger than the length of the input interval. We prove the following estimate.

<a id="stmt-prop-large-prime-tail"></a>
<div id="prop:large-prime-tail" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4**. *Let $f\in\mathbb Z[x]$ be primitive and irreducible, with positive leading coefficient and degree $4\le d\le8$. Put $k=d-2$. There exists $\delta=\delta_f>0$ such that $$\#\{n\in\mathbb Z:X<n\le2X,\ p^k\mid f(n)
          \text{ for some prime }p>X\}
 \ll_f X^{1-\delta}.$$*

</div>

The proposition does not require local admissibility. In Section <a href="#sec:sieve" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sieve">2</a>, we prove explicitly that it implies Theorem <a href="#thm:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:density">1</a>, including the stated sign and content generality. The density argument first holds the finite-prime cutoff fixed while $X$ tends to infinity, and then lets the cutoff tend to infinity. This order gives the exact Euler product without an assertion of a power-saving error in the density asymptotic.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Proof strategy

Fix a dyadic prime interval $P\le p\le2P$, and choose one such prime for each input being counted. Write $P=X^\eta$ and $b=d-k\eta$. The elementary relation $f(n)=yp^k$ puts the chosen triples on the surface $f(x)=yz^k$, with coordinate sizes $X,X^b,X^\eta$. A weighted determinant argument on this surface suffices when $\eta$ is large. The harder range requires more information from the factorization of $f$.

Let $\theta$ be a root of $f$ and let $K=\mathbb Q(\theta)$. Ideal factorization and the unit theorem produce integral elements $\alpha,\beta\in K$ satisfying $$\alpha^k\beta=\mu(n-\theta)$$ for a fixed nonzero integer $\mu$. Their conjugates are balanced: the sizes of $\alpha$ and $\beta$ at each embedding are respectively comparable to $P^{1/d}$ and $X^{b/d}$. Integer coordinates in a fixed integral basis therefore give two boxes, while their projective directions satisfy explicit polynomial equations. Near a balanced point, and after choosing one of finitely many local branches, either direction determines the other analytically once $1/n$ is specified.

We partition these directions into small paired boxes. An archimedean determinant gives a polynomial equation on the points in each pair. Components defined by an equation in only one of the two coordinate groups receive a second cut. Explicit complete intersection calculations measure the independent monomials available on the resulting sets. The distinction between a mixed equation and a pure equation is important: the latter also reduces the number of independent Taylor monomials in the local determinant.

Two further steps turn these cuts into a count. First, a reduced basis of the integer lattice associated with a direction box makes its points smaller in new integral coordinates. An exceptionally short lattice vector also limits the number of boxes in which it can occur. Second, we apply the general affine counting theorem proved in Section <a href="#sec:affine-counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:affine-counting">3</a>, using lower bounds for weighted spaces of polynomial functions. Its proof repeatedly uses residue classes at small auxiliary primes. Its auxiliary varieties depend on earlier choices of primes; a random deletion argument controls the primes at which their equations fail to give smooth local coordinates. This permits a uniform count through successive dimension drops.

For quartics, surfaces and curves need sharper geometric bounds. On a surface, the difficult projections have degree one or two. The quadric case supplies an additional field degree. On a plane, a deficient bound would force either a polynomial of degree at most four on a general line or a degree-two rational map on the line of directions to have four distinct critical values. On a curve, the limiting case is a polynomial of degree five with four prescribed critical values. Up to a normalization of two critical points, only finitely many such polynomials occur. Their integer values in the input interval form one set of size $O_f(X^{1/5})$, which is removed once before summing over boxes. The remaining curve estimates and an exact finite choice of parameters give a common power saving.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Organization

Figure <a href="#fig:proof-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-flow">1</a> shows how the principal estimates feed into the density theorem. Its arrows indicate uses of established modules; the order of the sections places the uniform counting statement before its arithmetic applications.

<figure id="fig:proof-flow">

<figcaption>The number-field route to the large-prime bound and the exact density. The direct-surface estimate handles the complementary upper prime range in Section <a href="#sec:tail-proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tail-proof">11</a>.</figcaption>
</figure>

Section <a href="#sec:sieve" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sieve">2</a> gives the density reduction, and Section <a href="#sec:affine-counting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:affine-counting">3</a> proves the general counting theorem. Sections <a href="#sec:arithmetic-boxes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arithmetic-boxes">4</a>–<a href="#sec:lattice-boxes" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:lattice-boxes">6</a> construct the number-field boxes, determinant cuts and lattice coordinates.
